There should be no 'Other' option, that will be provided automatically.","minItems":2,"maxItems":4,"type":"array","items":{"type":"object","properties":{"label":{"description":"The display text for this option that the user will see and select. Should be concise (1-5 words) and clearly describe the choice.","type":"string"},"description":{"description":"Explanation of what this option means or what will happen if chosen. Useful for providing context about trade-offs or implications.","type":"string"},"preview":{"description":"Optional preview content rendered when this option is focused. Use for mockups, code snippets, or visual comparisons that help users compare options. See the tool description for the expected content format.","type":"string"}},"required":["label","description"],"additionalProperties":false}},"multiSelect":{"description":"Set to true to allow the user to select multiple options instead of just one. Use when choices are not mutually exclusive.","default":false,"type":"boolean"}},"required":["question","header","options","multiSelect"],"additionalProperties":false}},"answers":{"description":"User answers collected by the permission component","type":"object","propertyNames":{"type":"string"},"additionalProperties":{"type":"string"}},"annotations":{"description":"Optional per-question annotations from the user (e.g., notes on preview selections). Keyed by question text.","type":"object","propertyNames":{"type":"string"},"additionalProperties":{"type":"object","properties":{"preview":{"description":"The preview content of the selected option, if the question used previews.","type":"string"},"notes":{"description":"Free-text notes the user added to their selection.","type":"string"}},"additionalProperties":false}},"metadata":{"description":"Optional metadata for tracking and analytics purposes. Not displayed to user.","type":"object","properties":{"source":{"description":"Optional identifier for the source of this question (e.g., \"remember\" for /remember command). Used for analytics tracking.","type":"string"}},"additionalProperties":false}},"required":["questions"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Bash","description":"Executes a bash command and returns its output.\n\n- Working directory persists between calls, but prefer absolute paths — `cd` in a compound command can trigger a permission prompt. Shell state (env vars, functions) does not persist; the shell is initialized from the user's profile.\n- IMPORTANT: Avoid using this tool to run `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `sed`, `awk`, or `echo` commands, unless explicitly instructed or after you have verified that a dedicated tool cannot accomplish your task. Instead, use the appropriate dedicated tool as this will provide a much better experience for the user.\n- Command output is displayed to you, not reliably to the user.\n- `timeout` is in milliseconds: default 120000, max 600000.\n- `run_in_background` runs the command detached: it keeps running across turns and re-invokes you when it exits. No `&` needed. Foreground `sleep` is blocked; use Monitor with an until-loop to wait on a condition.\n\n# Git\n- Interactive flags (`-i`, e.g. `git rebase -i`, `git add -i`) are not supported in this environment.\n- Use the `gh` CLI for GitHub operations (PRs, issues, API).\n- Commit or push only when the user asks. If on the default branch, branch first.\n- End git commit messages and PR bodies with the attribution lines given in the conversation's system-reminder, when one is present.","schema":{"name":"Bash","description":"Executes a bash command and returns its output.\n\n- Working directory persists between calls, but prefer absolute paths — `cd` in a compound command can trigger a permission prompt. Shell state (env vars, functions) does not persist; the shell is initialized from the user's profile.\n- IMPORTANT: Avoid using this tool to run `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `sed`, `awk`, or `echo` commands, unless explicitly instructed or after you have verified that a dedicated tool cannot accomplish your task. Instead, use the appropriate dedicated tool as this will provide a much better experience for the user.\n- Command output is displayed to you, not reliably to the user.\n- `timeout` is in milliseconds: default 120000, max 600000.\n- `run_in_background` runs the command detached: it keeps running across turns and re-invokes you when it exits. No `&` needed. Foreground `sleep` is blocked; use Monitor with an until-loop to wait on a condition.\n\n# Git\n- Interactive flags (`-i`, e.g. `git rebase -i`, `git add -i`) are not supported in this environment.\n- Use the `gh` CLI for GitHub operations (PRs, issues, API).\n- Commit or push only when the user asks. If on the default branch, branch first.\n- End git commit messages and PR bodies with the attribution lines given in the conversation's system-reminder, when one is present.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"command":{"description":"The command to execute","type":"string"},"timeout":{"description":"Optional timeout in milliseconds (max 600000)","type":"number"},"description":{"description":"Clear, concise description of what this command does in active voice. Never use words like \"complex\" or \"risk\" in the description - just describe what it does.\n\nSay what the command does in plain words: do not echo the command's text, its flags, or file paths - the user reads this description, often without seeing the command.\n\nFor simple commands (git, npm, standard CLI tools), keep it brief (5-10 words):\n- ls → \"List files in current directory\"\n- git status → \"Show working tree status\"\n- npm install → \"Install package dependencies\"\n\nFor commands that are harder to parse at a glance (piped commands, obscure flags, etc.), add enough context to clarify what it does:\n- find . -name \"*.tmp\" -exec rm {} \\; → \"Find and delete all .tmp files recursively\"\n- git reset --hard origin/main → \"Discard all local changes and match remote main\"\n- curl -s url | jq '.data[]' → \"Fetch JSON from URL and extract data array elements\"","type":"string"},"run_in_background":{"description":"Set to true to run this command in the background.","type":"boolean"},"dangerouslyDisableSandbox":{"description":"Set this to true to dangerously override sandbox mode and run commands without sandboxing.","type":"boolean"}},"required":["command"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Edit","description":"Performs exact string replacement in a file.\n\n- You must Read the file in this conversation before editing, or the call will fail.\n- `old_string` must match the file exactly, including indentation, and be unique — the edit fails otherwise. Strip the Read line prefix (line number + tab) before matching.\n- `replace_all: true` replaces every occurrence instead.","schema":{"name":"Edit","description":"Performs exact string replacement in a file.\n\n- You must Read the file in this conversation before editing, or the call will fail.\n- `old_string` must match the file exactly, including indentation, and be unique — the edit fails otherwise. Strip the Read line prefix (line number + tab) before matching.\n- `replace_all: true` replaces every occurrence instead.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"file_path":{"description":"The absolute path to the file to modify","type":"string"},"old_string":{"description":"The text to replace","type":"string"},"new_string":{"description":"The text to replace it with (must be different from old_string)","type":"string"},"replace_all":{"description":"Replace all occurrences of old_string (default false)","default":false,"type":"boolean"}},"required":["file_path","old_string","new_string"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ListAgents","description":"Lists agents you can SendMessage to — in-process subagents you spawned, the teammates on your team, other local Claude sessions on this machine, your Claude sessions running in the cloud (when this session has cloud access; a cloud session receives your message but cannot message any session back yet — do not ask it to reply, read its answer in its own transcript), and (when Remote Control is connected here) your account's other sessions — Remote Control sessions on other machines and cloud sessions, each row labeled by kind. Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate.","schema":{"name":"ListAgents","description":"Lists agents you can SendMessage to — in-process subagents you spawned, the teammates on your team, other local Claude sessions on this machine, your Claude sessions running in the cloud (when this session has cloud access; a cloud session receives your message but cannot message any session back yet — do not ask it to reply, read its answer in its own transcript), and (when Remote Control is connected here) your account's other sessions — Remote Control sessions on other machines and cloud sessions, each row labeled by kind. Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"channel":{"description":"Not available in this build; leave unset.","type":"string","maxLength":256},"q":{"description":"Not available in this build; leave unset.","type":"string","maxLength":256}},"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Read","description":"Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you.","schema":{"name":"Read","description":"Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"file_path":{"description":"The absolute path to the file to read","type":"string"},"offset":{"description":"The line number to start reading from. Only applicable to PDF files. Maximum 20 pages per request.","type":"string"}},"required":["file_path"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ReportFindings","description":"Report code-review findings as a typed list so the host UI can render them. Use this only when the active code-review instructions tell you to report findings with this tool; otherwise follow whatever output format those instructions specify. When reporting a review's results, call it once with the verified findings ranked most-severe first (empty array if nothing survived verification) and do not also print the findings as text. When re-reporting after applying fixes (only if the apply instructions ask for it), set `outcome` on each finding to what actually happened.","schema":{"name":"ReportFindings","description":"Report code-review findings as a typed list so the host UI can render them. empty if none survived","maxItems":32,"type":"array","items":{"type":"object","properties":{"file":{"description":"Repo-relative path of the file the finding is in","type":"string"},"line":{"description":"1-indexed line the finding anchors to","type":"integer","minimum":-9007199254740991,"maximum":9007199254740991},"summary":{"description":"One-sentence statement of the defect","type":"string"},"short_summary":{"description":"Compressed label for compact UI (≤60 chars): the claim alone, no rationale or consequence clause","type":"string","maxLength":60},"failure_scenario":{"description":"Concrete inputs/state → wrong output/crash","type":"string"},"category":{"description":"Short kebab-case slug of the finding type, e.g. \"correctness\", \"simplification\", \"efficiency\", \"test-coverage\"","type":"string","maxLength":40},"verdict":{"description":"Set when a verify pass ran; absent on inline-only reviews","type":"string","enum":["CONFIRMED","PLAUSIBLE"]},"outcome":{"description":"Set ONLY when re-reporting after applying fixes: what happened to this finding","type":"string","enum":["fixed","skipped","no_change_needed"]}},"required":["file","summary","failure_scenario"],"additionalProperties":false}}},"required":["findings"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ScheduleWakeup","description":"Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. The exception is external work the harness cannot track (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue) — there, pick a delay matched to how fast that state actually changes.\n\nPass the same /loop prompt back via `prompt` each turn so the next firing repeats the task. For an autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` as `prompt` instead — the runtime resolves it back to the autonomous-loop instructions at fire time. (There is a similar `<<autonomous-loop>>` sentinel for CronCreate-based autonomous loops; do not confuse the two — ScheduleWakeup always uses the `-dynamic` variant.) To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\nSet `noop: true` if nothing changed — you checked and there's nothing to report (\"no change\", \"still waiting\", \"quiet hold\"). Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. Omit `noop` when stopping (`stop: true`).\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use a 1-hour Anthropic prompt-cache TTL, so effectively every allowed delay (the runtime clamps to [60, 3600]) wakes up with your conversation context still cached. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n","schema":{"name":"ScheduleWakeup","description":"Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. It is never sent without the user's explicit approval, and calling this tool renders no UI and does not interrupt the conversation, so never announce it or ask the user about it mid-task.\n\nWrite `details` as short labeled bullets in this exact order, one to three lines each, no narrative paragraphs:\n- **What happened:** the observed behavior vs. what was expected, with exact error text if short. Facts only.\n- **What the user said:** the user's own words that prompted this, quoted. If nothing did, write \"User didn't comment; observed by the model.\" Never paraphrase sentiment into a stronger claim.\n- **Repro:** the minimal steps or shape that reproduces it.\n- **Evidence:** identifiers a reader can chase, such as request IDs, timestamps, file paths, versions. Omit the bullet if there are none.\n\nConstraints:\n- Never fabricate or exaggerate user sentiment; report only what actually happened.\n- Everything in the draft must be sourced from the user or the session, never inferred: leave unknown fields blank rather than guess, and add a final **Cause:** bullet only for a root cause you verified in-session.\n- Use `area` to name the part of Claude Code the feedback is about (a feature, command, or workflow, e.g. \"hooks config\", \"/help\", \"file editing\") when there is a clear one; leave it blank otherwise.\n- Use `failure_mode` ONLY when the report is about model behavior (how Claude responded), not a product bug. Omit only if genuinely unclear.\n- Do not include secrets or credentials. Refer to people by role (\"a teammate\", \"the PR reviewer\"), never by name, email address, or chat/user ID. This applies inside quoted user words too: replace a name or handle with a bracketed role (e.g. \"[a teammate]\") and keep the rest verbatim. {"parentUuid":"86af7096-f384-4426-8e2c-fb33757aaaf1","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"7991d629-f81d-402f-9460-494c333b2e9f","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EJdmyAXB32knkHu3xzzpWE","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"body\":\"## Parent\\n\\nSpec: #1\\n\\n## What to build\\n\\nAn Admin or Editor can sign in and out of the admin console. The first Admin is created from the command line with a password read from the environment, and a reproducible demo Editor account exists too, so later tickets can demonstrate Editor behaviour before user management is built. Roles (Admin, Editor) and the active/Deactivated state exist on every account from the start.\\n\\n## Acceptance criteria\\n\\n- [ ] A `cms` command creates the first Admin account. The password comes from the environment and is never printed, logged or committed.\\n- [ ] The demo seed creates one Admin and one Editor, both with a public Display name, with passwords from the environment. The same demo accounts are available to tests through the existing `client_as(\\\"admin\\\")` and `client_as(\\\"editor\\\")` fixtures, which must log in successfully (the fixture currently posts an email-style identifier; reconcile the sign-in identifier and the fixture on purpose).\\n- [ ] Sign-in accepts a correct username/password and sends the user to the dashboard placeholder. A wrong password or unknown username shows one generic message that does not reveal which was wrong.\\n- [ ] Passwords are stored hashed with argon2. No response, log or database row contains a plain password.\\n- [ ] A Deactivated account cannot sign in, and an existing session for it stops working.\\n- [ ] Sign-out ends the session.\\n- [ ] Any admin address, visited while signed out, sends the visitor to sign-in.\\n- [ ] Every state-changing form (including sign-in and sign-out) carries a CSRF token, and a request without a valid token is rejected.\\n- [ ] `.env.example` lists every variable needed; `.env`, database files and secrets stay out of Git.\\n- [ ] Tests at the admin HTTP seam cover sign-in success and failure, Deactivated accounts, sign-out, redirects when signed out, and CSRF rejection. A small `cms` smoke test covers first-Admin creation from an environment password.\\n- [ ] The existing T00 tests still pass.\\n\\n## Blocked by\\n\\nNone (can start {"parentUuid":"796d0bfe-f9e6-4d3e-9683-c079ff6c4a2e","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"7991d629-f81d-402f-9460-494c333b2e9f","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PrvEWA3fpjTWVmY5HuTCnT","type":"tool_result","content":"## Problem Statement\n\nASA, the African Student Association at Kenyon, is described in the client brief as a student group primarily for African students and students with African backgrounds, while welcoming anyone interested in African cultures and community. Students need an easy way to find event announcements (including an Afrobeat event), learn about the association, explore cultural resources, and see photos from past activities. Its leaders need event announcements to be quick to publish, information about ASA to stay easy to find in the navigation, and a way to share photos only when ASA has permission to use them. One ASA leader should manage accounts and permanent pages; another member should be able to write and update event posts without a complicated form.\n\nThis is a class project (IPHS 400, Mini-Project #2). No claim is made about any real ASA process, officers, schedule or history. Terms below are defined in `CONTEXT.md`.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS. A local admin console lets an Admin and Editors write **Posts** and **Pages**, manage accounts, and preview content. A `cms publish` step renders only **Published** content into a static public site, and `cms deploy` sends it to GitHub Pages, at which point the content is **Live**. The admin console never goes on the public internet (ADR-001).\n\nThe public site has a **Home page** (a short **Welcome text** taken from the client brief plus the five latest **Announcements**), an **Announcements** list, and **Navigation** built from published Pages (starter Pages for the demo: About ASA, Resources, Photos). Event announcements are ordinary Posts with the event's date, time, location and who can attend written in the **Body**. Photos are images inside Posts or Pages; there is no upload form.\n\n## User Stories\n\n### Signing in\n1. As an Admin, I want to sign in with a username and password, so that only authorised people can change the site.\n2. As an Editor, I want to sign in and out, so that my session ends when I leave a shared computer.\n3. As an account owner, I want my password stored hashed, so that nobody, including the Admin, can read it.\n4. As an Admin, I want a wrong password to be rejected with a clear message that does not reveal whether the username exists, so that accounts cannot be guessed.\n5. As someone with a Deactivated account, I want sign-in to be refused, so that a former member can no longer change the site.\n6. As a signed-in user, I want every form I submit to be protected against forged requests, so that another website cannot act as me.\n7. As a visitor who is not signed in, I want any admin address to send me to sign-in, so that I never see admin content.\n\n### First Admin and setup\n8. As the project owner, I want the first Admin account created from the command line with a password taken from the environment, so that no password is committed to Git.\n9. As the project owner, I want a clean clone to run from `.env.example` plus a few documented steps, so that a grader can reproduce the demo.\n10. As the project owner, I want the database and `.env` kept out of Git, so that private data is never published.\n11. As the project owner, I want demo data that can be rebuilt on demand, so that screenshots and tests are repeatable.\n\n### Roles and permission refusal\n12. As an Admin, I want to manage accounts and permanent Pages, so that I control who can change the site and what the Navigation contains.\n13. As an Editor, I want to create and publish Posts directly, so that an announcement can go out quickly.\n14. As an Editor, I want to edit any Post, including another Editor's, so that event details can be corrected close to the event if the writer is unavailable.\n15. As an Editor, I want to update the Body of an existing Page, so that the Photos page can stay current.\n16. As an Admin, I want only Admins to delete Posts, so that a mistaken deletion is unlikely.\n17. As an Admin, I want only Admins to create, delete, publish, unpublish, retitle or re-slug a Page, so that Navigation and links stay stable.\n18. As an Admin, I want only Admins to change a slug, and to be warned that doing so can break an old link, so that shared links keep working.\n19. As an Editor, I want admin-only controls hidden from my screens, so that my screens stay simple.\n20. As an Editor who reaches an Admin-only address or submits a forbidden action directly, I want a clear \"You don't have permission\" page with a link back to the dashboard, so that I understand what happened.\n21. As an Admin, I want every permission enforced by the server, not by hidden buttons, so that access control is real.\n\n### Posts\n22. As an Editor, I want to write a Post with a title and a Body and save it as a Draft, so that I can finish it later.\n23. As an Editor, I want to mark a Post Published, so that it is ready for the public site.\n24. As an Editor, I want to unpublish a Post, so that a wrong announcement stops going out.\n25. As an Editor, I want the slug generated from my title on first save, so that I never have to think about URLs.\n26. As an Editor, I want a number added automatically when a slug already exists, so that two Posts never collide.\n27. As an Editor, I want the slug to stay unchanged when I correct the title or event details, so that shared links keep working.\n28. As an Editor, I want a short hint reminding me to include What, When, Where and Who can attend, so that announcements are complete.\n29. As an Editor, I want the hint not pre-filled into the Body and hideable without losing content, so that I can write naturally.\n30. As an Editor, I want the Post to record me as its Author automatically, so that I do not fill in extra fields.\n31. As an Editor, I want the Post's created, updated and first-published times recorded automatically, so that dates are trustworthy.\n\n### Pages\n32. As an Admin, I want to create a Page with a title, slug and Body, so that permanent information has a home.\n33. As an Admin, I want a published Page to appear in the public Navigation automatically, so that visitors can find it.\n34. As an Admin, I want an unpublished Page to disappear from the Navigation, so that unfinished pages are not linked.\n35. As an Admin, I want to delete a Page, so that obsolete pages can be removed.\n36. As an Editor, I want to edit the Body of a Page but see title, slug, status and delete controls as unavailable, so that I cannot break the Navigation.\n\n### Writing, preview and images\n37. As an Editor, I want to use headings, emphasis, lists, links and images with alt text, so that announcements read well without learning HTML.\n38. As an Editor, I want raw HTML, scripts, frames and forms in my text stripped, so that the site is safe.\n39. As an Editor, I want a preview that shows exactly what the public page will render, so that there are no surprises after publishing.\n40. As an Editor, I want to write a caption, alt text and Markdown reference for an image, so that photos are accessible.\n41. As an Editor, I understand I cannot add a new image file myself, so that the release-holder can confirm Photo permission first.\n42. As the release-holder, I want approved image files copied into the public site with relative paths, so that photos show up after deployment.\n43. As the release-holder, I want demo illustrations labelled as illustrative and never presented as photos of real ASA events, so that visitors are not misled.\n\n### Admin console\n44. As an Admin, I want a dashboard with the counts of Published Posts, Draft Posts, Published Pages and Draft Pages, so that I see the state of the site at a glance.\n45. As an Admin, I want the dashboard to also show the number of active and Deactivated accounts, so that I can keep track of who has access.\n46. As an Editor, I want the dashboard to show the four content counts but no account counts, so that I only see what I need.\n47. As any signed-in user, I want a reminder on the dashboard that marking content Published does not make it Live until `cms publish` and `cms deploy` run, so that I do not expect instant changes.\n48. As any signed-in user, I want one Content list I can filter by Post or Page and by Draft or Published, so that I can find content without opening each item.\n49. As any signed-in user, I want each row to show title, status, Display name and last-updated time, so that I can tell items apart.\n50. As a phone user, I want the admin console usable at phone width, so that I can post from anywhere.\n\n### User management\n51. As an Admin, I want to create an account with a username, a private password, a role and a public Display name, so that a new member can start writing.\n52. As an Admin, I want to change an account's role, so that responsibilities can change.\n53. As an Admin, I want to deactivate an account, so that a former member can no longer sign in while their published Posts stay.\n54. As an Admin, I want to reactivate a deactivated account, so that a mistake is recoverable.\n55. As an Admin, I want to set a new password for an existing account without seeing the old one, so that a lost password can be recovered.\n56. As an Admin, I want a reset password never printed or logged, so that it cannot leak.\n57. As an Admin, I want to change an account's Display name, so that a former member can ask for their name to be replaced with \"ASA Events Team\".\n58. As an Admin, I want the original Author link of every Post kept, even after a Display name change or deactivation, so that the record stays accurate.\n59. As an Admin, I want the private username never used as a byline by default, so that private account names do not appear publicly.\n60. As an Admin, I want accounts deactivated rather than deleted, so that history is preserved.\n\n### Public site\n61. As a first-time visitor, I want a short welcome on the Home page, so that I know what ASA is and that I am welcome.\n62. As a visitor, I want the five latest Announcements on the Home page with a link to all of them, so that I can see what is new.\n63. As a visitor, I want an Announcements page listing all Published Posts, newest first by first-published date, so that I can browse.\n64. As a visitor, I want each Announcement as a clear Event card showing its title, first-published date and byline, so that I can scan quickly on a phone.\n65. As a visitor, I want an \"Updated\" date on a card when the content changed after first publishing, so that I know details were corrected.\n66. As a visitor, I want the event's actual date, time, location and who can attend in the Body, and the publication date never labelled as the event date, so that I am not misled.\n67. As a visitor, I want a Post to keep its original first-published date if it is unpublished and later published again, so that the order is stable.\n68. As a visitor, I want the Navigation to list Announcements and every published Page (About ASA, Resources, Photos for the demo), so that information is easy to find.\n69. As a visitor, I want the Photos page to say that actual event photos will be added when ASA has permission to use them, so that placeholders are not mistaken for real events.\n70. As a visitor, I want the public site to work on a phone with readable text, so that I can use it anywhere.\n71. As a visitor who uses a keyboard or screen reader, I want a sensible heading order, readable contrast, visible focus, keyboard access to all controls, and alt text on every image, so that the site is accessible.\n72. As a visitor, I want Drafts and unpublished content never to appear anywhere in the public site, so that unfinished work stays private.\n73. As a visitor, I want the site to work from a GitHub Pages subfolder, so that no link or style is broken.\n\n### Releasing\n74. As the release-holder, I want `cms publish` to write the public site from Published content only, so that Drafts never leave the database.\n75. As the release-holder, I want `cms deploy` to send the result to GitHub Pages, so that content becomes Live.\n76. As the release-holder, I want a short release checklist covering publish, deploy, checking relative paths, adding approved images, and confirming Photo permission, so that the step is quick and repeatable.\n77. As the release-holder, I want the checklist to state that an Editor cannot add an image file alone, so that clients are not surprised.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n- **Architecture is fixed** by ADR-001 (local admin console, static public site) and ADR-002 (FastAPI, Jinja2, SQLite, argon2, Markdown sanitizer). No new dependencies.\n- **Content model.** Two kinds of content only: Post and Page. No separate Event type and no event-date field. A Post has a title, slug, Markdown Body, status (Draft or Published), Author, and created, updated and first-published times. A Page has the same fields except that the Author is optional for display and a Page does not appear in the Announcements list.\n- **First-published date** is set the first time a Post becomes Published and never cleared, including when the Post is unpublished and republished. The **Updated date** on the public card is shown when the Body or title changes after the first-published date.\n- **Ordering.** Announcements are ordered by first-published date, newest first. Editing does not reorder. The Home page shows the five most recent; the Announcements page shows all Published Posts.\n- **Slugs.** A Post's slug is generated from its title on first save, made unique by adding a number, and never changed automatically. A Page's slug is entered by the Admin. Only the Admin can change any slug, and the form warns that this can break existing links. Slug edits are not shown to Editors.\n- **Accounts.** An account has a private username, a password hash, a role (Admin or Editor), a public Display name, and an active flag. There is no account deletion. Posts keep their Author link permanently. A Post's public byline is the Author's current Display name. The username is never used as a byline.\n- **Authentication.** Session cookie signed by the server. Passwords are hashed with argon2 and are never stored, shown or logged in plain form. Sign-in failures use one generic message. A Deactivated account cannot sign in and any existing session for it stops working.\n- **Authorization.** A single central rule table maps each action to the roles allowed. Every state-changing route checks it on the server. Templates hide controls the current role cannot use, but hiding is never the protection. A refused action shows a \"You don't have permission\" page with a link to the dashboard, and a not-signed-in visitor is sent to sign-in.\n- **Permission matrix.** Admin: everything. Editor: sign in and out; create, edit and publish or unpublish Posts, including other people's; edit the Body of an existing Page; view dashboard counts and the Content list. Editor cannot: delete Posts or Pages; create, publish, unpublish, retitle or re-slug Pages; change any slug; open user management or change any account, role or Display name; reset passwords.\n- **CSRF.** Every state-changing form carries a token that the server verifies.\n- **Markdown and sanitizing.** Allowed: headings, emphasis, lists, links and images with alt text. Raw HTML, scripts, iframes and forms are stripped. The same rendering and sanitizing path is used for the admin preview and the public export, so the preview matches the public page.\n- **Images.** No upload feature. Approved image files are added to the project by the release-holder and copied into the public site by `cms publish`. Markdown references use relative paths. Demo illustrations are clearly labelled illustrative.\n- **Admin console screens.** Sign-in, Dashboard, Content list, Post editor with Preview and the Event details hint, Page editor (reduced controls for Editors), User management (Admin only), and the permission-refusal page. Layouts work at phone width.\n- **Dashboard.** Four content counts for everyone; active and deactivated account counts for the Admin only; a reminder that Published is not Live. No \"Changes not live yet\" indicator.\n- **Content list.** One list of Posts and Pages, filterable by type and by status, with title, status, Display name and last-updated time on each row.\n- **Event details hint.** A short, dismissible hint near the Post editor listing What, When, Where and Who can attend. It is never inserted into the Body.\n- **Publishing.** `cms publish` renders Home, Announcements, each Published Post, each Published Page and the Navigation to plain HTML, using only Published content and only relative paths. It copies approved image files. `cms deploy` pushes the result to the `gh-pages` branch. Nothing is Live until both have run.\n- **Public design.** Polished, mobile-first, readable text, clear Event cards, a few appropriately licensed images or decorative graphics that are never presented as real ASA event photos. Accessibility requirements: sensible heading order, readable contrast, visible focus and full keyboard access, phone-width layout, and alt text on every image.\n- **Content carried from the client brief.** The Welcome text uses only the brief's wording. Starter Pages (About ASA, Resources, Photos) and seed Posts are demo placeholders and contain no claims about ASA's real history, officers, members, schedule or approval process.\n- **First Admin.** Created from the command line at setup, with the password read from the environment. No password, `.env` or database file is committed. A clean clone runs from `.env.example` plus documented steps.\n- **Demo data.** The seed data is rebuilt on demand and includes both roles, published and draft Posts and Pages, and a deactivated account.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n- **A good test checks external behaviour only:** what a signed-in user sees and can do, and what files the publish step writes. It does not check internal functions, the database layout or template details.\n- **Seam 1: the admin console over HTTP.** Use the existing `client` and `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")` fixtures. Cover sign-in and sign-out, Deactivated accounts, each row of the permission matrix (both the allowed and the refused case, for every Admin-only action), CSRF rejection, Post and Page create/read/update/delete, slug generation and stability, First-published date rules, Dashboard counts by role, Content list filtering, Preview matching public output, user management and Password reset, and Display name changes.\n- **Seam 2: the published site on disk.** Call the publish step against seeded data into a temporary folder and read the HTML files. Cover: Drafts and unpublished content absent; sanitized Markdown (raw HTML, scripts, iframes and forms stripped); Navigation built from published Pages; Announcements ordering and the five-item Home page; byline uses the Display name and never the username; \"Updated\" date shown after later edits; only relative `href` and `src` values; approved image files copied; the Welcome text present.\n- **Seam 3 (small smoke test): the `cms` command.** First-Admin creation from an environment password, and a clean-setup run from `.env.example`. Kept small and separate.\n- **Prior art.** `tests/conftest.py` already provides the app and `client_as` fixtures, and the walking-skeleton test shows the style. Access-control tests should follow the one-line pattern in the `conftest.py` docstring.\n- **Test-first.** Each ticket starts with failing tests. Read the tests before letting them pass, to catch a test written against a misunderstanding.\n- **Security checks.** Drafts never appear in the export, no plain password in any response or log, no root-absolute paths in the export, sanitized output, CSRF on every state-changing form, and server-side enforcement of every Admin-only action.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- A separate Event content type, an event-date field, and automatic upcoming/past sorting.\n- Photo upload forms, automatic gallery management, and photo approvals enforced by software. (The Photos page with approved images is in scope.)\n- Account deletion, and a first-login password change flow.\n- Email sending, including email-based password reset.\n- A \"Changes not live yet\" indicator.\n- RSVPs and sign-ups, comments, search, and tags or categories.\n- Multi-site support, drag-and-drop page building, and a rich-text WYSIWYG editor.\n- Any claim about ASA's real history, members, leaders, schedule or approval process.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- **Release checklist.** Written as part of the project. It covers running publish and deploy, the relative-path check, adding approved image files, confirming Photo permission before a real photo is added, and the limit that an Editor cannot add an image file alone. For the class demo the project author runs it from their own laptop. ASA has no known person responsible for this step, and none is assumed.\n- **Photo permission** is a working rule for this project, not a known ASA policy.\n- **ADRs.** No new ADR was needed from the grill. Add one later only if a decision is hard to reverse and surprising.\n- **Vocabulary.** Use the terms in `CONTEXT.md` (Post, Announcements, Event announcement, Page, Published, Live, Display name, Username, Deactivated account, Permission refusal, and so on). Do not use \"Upcoming Events\" for the Announcements list.\n\n----\n.claude/hooks/ctx_compact_log.py\n.claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py\n.claude/hooks/ctx_statusline.py\n.claude/hooks/save_transcript.py\n.claude/settings.json\n.claude/skills/PINNED.md\n.env.example\n.gitignore\nCLAUDE.md\nCONTEXT.md\nREADME.md\nSECURITY-CHECKLIST.md\napp/__init__.py\napp/cli.py\napp/main.py\napp/publish.py\napp/settings.py\ndocs/adr/ADR-001-local-admin-static-public.md\ndocs/adr/ADR-002-stack.md\ndocs/agents/domain.md\ndocs/agents/issue-tracker.md\ndocs/handoff/.gitkeep\ndocs/manual_iphs400_mp2-web-cms_20260922.md\ndocs/mp2-grading-rubric_20260922.md\ndocs/mp2-setup_context-threshold-hook_20260922.md\ndocs/process/.gitkeep\nnotes/.gitkeep\nnotes/client-brief.md\nnotes/cms-field-notes.md\nnotes/setup_check.md\nnotes/token-budget-plan.md\nnotes/usage-ledger.csv\npyproject.toml\nscripts/check_submission.py\nscripts/seed_demo.py\nscripts/usage_report.py\ntemplates/admin/hello.html\ntemplates/base.html\ntemplates/public/home.html\ntests/conftest.py\ntests/test_ctx_guard.py\ntests/test_t00.py\ntests/test_usage_report.py\nuv.lock\n----\n[project]\nname = \"iphs400-mp2-cms\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ndescription = \"IPHS 400 MP2 — a small web CMS with a local admin console and a static public site\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.12\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"fastapi>=0.115\",\n    \"uvicorn[standard]>=0.32\",\n    \"jinja2>=3.1\",\n    \"python-multipart>=0.0.12\",   # form posts\n    \"itsdangerous>=2.2\",          # signed session cookies\n    \"argon2-cffi>=23.1\",          # password hashing (never store plain passwords)\n    \"markdown-it-py>=3.0\",        # Markdown -> HTML\n    \"nh3>=0.2\",                   # HTML sanitizer (strips <script> etc.)\n    \"ghp-import>=2.1\",            # push site/ to the gh-pages branch\n]\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [\"pytest>=8.3\", \"httpx>=0.28\"]\n\n[project.scripts]\ncms = \"app.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\n\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"hatchling\"]\nbuild-backend = \"hatchling.build\"\n\n[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]\npackages = [\"app\"]\n# ADR-002: FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite, with no frontend framework\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nThe project has to be readable by someone who has never built a web application,\ngradable from a clean clone, and finishable in two weeks on a $20 plan. Options\nconsidered: Django (batteries included, but a large amount of generated code to\nread), Flask (similar to FastAPI here), a JavaScript stack (a second language and\na build step), and FastAPI with server-rendered templates.\n\n## Decision\n\nFastAPI with Jinja2 templates, SQLite for storage, argon2 for password hashing,\nand an HTML sanitizer for user Markdown. No frontend framework, no build step.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Every page is server-rendered, so \"view source\" shows what the code produced,\n  which makes debugging visible.\n- SQLite is a single file, so a grader can seed and inspect it easily. It is\n  gitignored; `scripts/seed_demo.py` rebuilds it.\n- No build step means the file you preview is the file that ships.\n- Interactive flourishes (live preview without reload, drag-and-drop) cost more\n  here than in a JavaScript stack. That is an accepted trade.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"a2aef8fe-48fd-46f3-bf3d-44778060ac42","timestamp":"2026-09-29T02:28:20.803Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"## Problem Statement\n\nASA, the African Student Association at Kenyon, is described in the client brief as a student group primarily for African students and students with African backgrounds, while welcoming anyone interested in African cultures and community. Students need an easy way to find event announcements (including an Afrobeat event), learn about the association, explore cultural resources, and see photos from past activities. Its leaders need event announcements to be quick to publish, information about ASA to stay easy to find in the navigation, and a way to share photos only when ASA has permission to use them. One ASA leader should manage accounts and permanent pages; another member should be able to write and update event posts without a complicated form.\n\nThis is a class project (IPHS 400, Mini-Project #2). No claim is made about any real ASA process, officers, schedule or history. Terms below are defined in `CONTEXT.md`.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS. A local admin console lets an Admin and Editors write **Posts** and **Pages**, manage accounts, and preview content. A `cms publish` step renders only **Published** content into a static public site, and `cms deploy` sends it to GitHub Pages, at which point the content is **Live**. The admin console never goes on the public internet (ADR-001).\n\nThe public site has a **Home page** (a short **Welcome text** taken from the client brief plus the five latest **Announcements**), an **Announcements** list, and **Navigation** built from published Pages (starter Pages for the demo: About ASA, Resources, Photos). Event announcements are ordinary Posts with the event's date, time, location and who can attend written in the **Body**. Photos are images inside Posts or Pages; there is no upload form.\n\n## User Stories\n\n### Signing in\n1. As an Admin, I want to sign in with a username and password, so that only authorised people can change the site.\n2. As an Editor, I want to sign in and out, so that my session ends when I leave a shared computer.\n3. As an account owner, I want my password stored hashed, so that nobody, including the Admin, can read it.\n4. As an Admin, I want a wrong password to be rejected with a clear message that does not reveal whether the username exists, so that accounts cannot be guessed.\n5. As someone with a Deactivated account, I want sign-in to be refused, so that a former member can no longer change the site.\n6. As a signed-in user, I want every form I submit to be protected against forged requests, so that another website cannot act as me.\n7. As a visitor who is not signed in, I want any admin address to send me to sign-in, so that I never see admin content.\n\n### First Admin and setup\n8. As the project owner, I want the first Admin account created from the command line with a password taken from the environment, so that no password is committed to Git.\n9. As the project owner, I want a clean clone to run from `.env.example` plus a few documented steps, so that a grader can reproduce the demo.\n10. As the project owner, I want the database and `.env` kept out of Git, so that private data is never published.\n11. As the project owner, I want demo data that can be rebuilt on demand, so that screenshots and tests are repeatable.\n\n### Roles and permission refusal\n12. As an Admin, I want to manage accounts and permanent Pages, so that I control who can change the site and what the Navigation contains.\n13. As an Editor, I want to create and publish Posts directly, so that an announcement can go out quickly.\n14. As an Editor, I want to edit any Post, including another Editor's, so that event details can be corrected close to the event if the writer is unavailable.\n15. As an Editor, I want to update the Body of an existing Page, so that the Photos page can stay current.\n16. As an Admin, I want only Admins to delete Posts, so that a mistaken deletion is unlikely.\n17. As an Admin, I want only Admins to create, delete, publish, unpublish, retitle or re-slug a Page, so that Navigation and links stay stable.\n18. As an Admin, I want only Admins to change a slug, and to be warned that doing so can break an old link, so that shared links keep working.\n19. As an Editor, I want admin-only controls hidden from my screens, so that my screens stay simple.\n20. As an Editor who reaches an Admin-only address or submits a forbidden action directly, I want a clear \"You don't have permission\" page with a link back to the dashboard, so that I understand what happened.\n21. As an Admin, I want every permission enforced by the server, not by hidden buttons, so that access control is real.\n\n### Posts\n22. As an Editor, I want to write a Post with a title and a Body and save it as a Draft, so that I can finish it later.\n23. As an Editor, I want to mark a Post Published, so that it is ready for the public site.\n24. As an Editor, I want to unpublish a Post, so that a wrong announcement stops going out.\n25. As an Editor, I want the slug generated from my title on first save, so that I never have to think about URLs.\n26. As an Editor, I want a number added automatically when a slug already exists, so that two Posts never collide.\n27. As an Editor, I want the slug to stay unchanged when I correct the title or event details, so that shared links keep working.\n28. As an Editor, I want a short hint reminding me to include What, When, Where and Who can attend, so that announcements are complete.\n29. As an Editor, I want the hint not pre-filled into the Body and hideable without losing content, so that I can write naturally.\n30. As an Editor, I want the Post to record me as its Author automatically, so that I do not fill in extra fields.\n31. As an Editor, I want the Post's created, updated and first-published times recorded automatically, so that dates are trustworthy.\n\n### Pages\n32. As an Admin, I want to create a Page with a title, slug and Body, so that permanent information has a home.\n33. As an Admin, I want a published Page to appear in the public Navigation automatically, so that visitors can find it.\n34. As any signed-in user, I want a reminder on the dashboard that marking content Published does not make it Live until `cms publish` and `cms deploy` run, so that I do not expect instant changes.\n48. As the release-holder, I want the checklist to state that an Editor cannot add an image file alone, so that clients are not surprised.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n- **Architecture is fixed** by ADR-001 (local admin console, static public site) and ADR-002 (FastAPI, Jinja2, SQLite, argon2, Markdown sanitizer). No new dependencies.\n- **Content model.** Two kinds of content only: Post and Page. No separate Event type and no event-date field. A Post has a title, slug, Markdown Body, status (Draft or Published), Author, and created, updated and first-published times. A Page has the same fields except that the Author is optional for display and a Page does not appear in the Announcements list.\n- **First-published date** is set the first time a Post becomes Published and never cleared, including when the Post is unpublished and republished. The **Updated date** on the public card is shown when the Body or title changes after the first-published date.\n- **Ordering.** Announcements are ordered by first-published date, newest first. Editing does not reorder. The Home page shows the five most recent; the Announcements page shows all Published Posts.\n- **Slugs.** A Post's slug is generated from its title on first save, made unique by adding a number, and never changed automatically. A Page's slug is entered by the Admin. Only the Admin can change any slug, and the form warns that this can break existing links. Slug edits are not shown to Editors.\n- **Accounts.** An account has a private username, a password hash, a role (Admin or Editor), a public Display name, and an active flag. There is no account deletion. Posts keep their Author link permanently. A Post's public byline is the Author's current Display name. The username is never used as a byline.\n- **Authentication.** Session cookie signed by the server. Passwords are hashed with argon2 and are never stored, shown or logged in plain form. Sign-in failures use one generic message. A Deactivated account cannot sign in and any existing session for it stops working.\n- **Authorization.** A single central rule table maps each action to the roles allowed. Every state-changing route checks it on the server. Templates hide controls the current role cannot use, but hiding is never the protection. A refused action shows a \"You don't have permission\" page with a link to the dashboard, and a not-signed-in visitor is sent to sign-in.\n- **Permission matrix.** Admin: everything. Editor: sign in and out; create, edit and publish or unpublish Posts, including other people's; edit the Body of an existing Page; view dashboard counts and the Content list. Editor cannot: delete Posts or Pages; create, publish, unpublish, retitle or re-slug Pages; change any slug; open user management or change any account, role or Display name; reset passwords.\n- **CSRF.** Every state-changing form carries a token that the server verifies.\n- **Markdown and sanitizing.** Allowed: headings, emphasis, lists, links and images with alt text. Raw HTML, scripts, iframes and forms are stripped. The same rendering and sanitizing path is used for the admin preview and the public export, so the preview matches the public page.\n- **Images.** No upload feature. Approved image files are added to the project by the release-holder and copied into the public site by `cms publish`. Markdown references use relative paths. Demo illustrations are clearly labelled illustrative.\n- **Admin console screens.** Sign-in, Dashboard, Content list, Post editor with Preview and the Event details hint, Page editor (reduced controls for Editors), User management (Admin only), and the permission-refusal page. Layouts work at phone width.\n- **Dashboard.** Four content counts for everyone; active and deactivated account counts for the Admin only; a reminder that Published is not Live. No \"Changes not live yet\" indicator.\n- **Content list.** One list of Posts and Pages, filterable by type and by status, with title, status, Display name and last-updated time on each row.\n- **Event details hint.** A short, dismissible hint near the Post editor listing What, When, Where and Who can attend. It is never inserted into the Body.\n- **Publishing.** `cms publish` renders Home, Announcements, each Published Post, each Published Page and the Navigation to plain HTML, using only Published content and only relative paths. It copies approved image files. `cms deploy` pushes the result to the `gh-pages` branch. Nothing is Live until both have run.\n- **Public design.** Polished, mobile-first, readable text, clear Event cards, a few appropriately licensed images or decorative graphics that are never presented as real ASA event photos. Accessibility requirements: sensible heading order, readable contrast, visible focus and full keyboard access, phone-width layout, and alt text on every image.\n- **Content carried from the client brief.** The Welcome text uses only the brief's wording. Starter Pages (About ASA, Resources, Photos) and seed Posts are demo placeholders and contain no claims about ASA's real history, officers, members, schedule or approval process.\n- **First Admin.** Created from the command line at setup, with the password read from the environment. No password, `.env` or database file is committed. A clean clone runs from `.env.example` plus documented steps.\n- **Demo data.** The seed data is rebuilt on demand and includes both roles, published and draft Posts and Pages, and a deactivated account.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n- **A good test checks external behaviour only:** what a signed-in user sees and can do, and what files the publish step writes. It does not check internal functions, the database layout or template details.\n- **Seam 1: the admin console over HTTP.** Use the existing `client` and `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")` fixtures. Cover sign-in and sign-out, Deactivated accounts, each row of the permission matrix (both the allowed and the refused case, for every Admin-only action), CSRF rejection, Post and Page create/read/update/delete, slug generation and stability, First-published date rules, Dashboard counts by role, Content list filtering, Preview matching public output, user management and Password reset, and Display name changes.\n- **Seam 2: the published site on disk.** Call the publish step against seeded data into a temporary folder and read the HTML files. Cover: Drafts and unpublished content absent; sanitized Markdown (raw HTML, scripts, iframes and forms stripped); Navigation built from published Pages; Announcements ordering and the five-item Home page; byline uses the Display name and never the username; \"Updated\" date shown after later edits; only relative `href` and `src` values; approved image files copied; the Welcome text present.\n- **Seam 3 (small smoke test): the `cms` command.** First-Admin creation from an environment password, and a clean-setup run from `.env.example`. Kept small and separate.\n- **Prior art.** `tests/conftest.py` already provides the app and `client_as` fixtures, and the walking-skeleton test shows the style. Access-control tests should follow the one-line pattern in the `conftest.py` docstring.\n- **Test-first.** Each ticket starts with failing tests. Read the tests before letting them pass, to catch a test written against a misunderstanding.\n- **Security checks.** Drafts never appear in the export, no plain password in any response or log, no root-absolute paths in the export, sanitized output, CSRF on every state-changing form, and server-side enforcement of every Admin-only action.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- A separate Event content type, an event-date field, and automatic upcoming/past sorting.\n- Photo upload forms, automatic gallery management, and photo approvals enforced by software. (The Photos page with approved images is in scope.)\n- Account deletion, and a first-login password change flow.\n- Email sending, including email-based password reset.\n- A \"Changes not live yet\" indicator.\n- RSVPs and sign-ups, comments, search, and tags or categories.\n- Multi-site support, drag-and-drop page building, and a rich-text WYSIWYG editor.\n- Any claim about ASA's real history, members, leaders, schedule or approval process.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- **Release checklist.** Written as part of the project. It covers running publish and deploy, the relative-path check, adding approved image files, confirming Photo permission before a real photo is added, and the limit that an Editor cannot add an image file alone. For the class demo the project author runs it from their own laptop. ASA has no known person responsible for this step, and none is assumed.\n- **Photo permission** is a working rule for this project, not a known ASA policy.\n- **ADRs.** No new ADR was needed from the grill. Add one later only if a decision is hard to reverse and surprising.\n- **Vocabulary.** Use the terms in `CONTEXT.md` (Post, Announcements, Event announcement, Page, Published, Live, Display name, Username, Deactivated account, Permission refusal, and so on). Do not use \"Upcoming Events\" for the Announcements list.\n\n----\n.claude/hooks/ctx_compact_log.py\n.claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py\n.claude/hooks/ctx_statusline.py\n.claude/hooks/save_transcript.py\n.claude/settings.json\n.claude/skills/PINNED.md\n.env.example\n.gitignore\nCLAUDE.md\nCONTEXT.md\nREADME.md\nSECURITY-CHECKLIST.md\napp/__init__.py\napp/cli.py\napp/main.py\napp/publish.py\napp/settings.py\ndocs/adr/ADR-001-local-admin-static-public.md\ndocs/adr/ADR-002-stack.md\ndocs/agents/domain.md\ndocs/agents/issue-tracker.md\ndocs/handoff/.gitkeep\ndocs/manual_iphs400_mp2-web-cms_20260922.md\ndocs/mp2-grading-rubric_20260922.md\ndocs/mp2-setup_context-threshold-hook_20260922.md\ndocs/process/.gitkeep\nnotes/.gitkeep\nnotes/client-brief.md\nnotes/cms-field-notes.md\nnotes/setup_check.md\nnotes/token-budget-plan.md\nnotes/usage-ledger.csv\npyproject.toml\nscripts/check_submission.py\nscripts/seed_demo.py\nscripts/usage_report.py\ntemplates/admin/hello.html\ntemplates/base.html\ntemplates/public/home.html\ntests/conftest.py\ntests/test_ctx_guard.py\ntests/test_t00.py\ntests/test_usage_report.py\nuv.lock\n----\n[project]\nname = \"iphs400-mp2-cms\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ndescription = \"IPHS 400 MP2 — a small web CMS with a local admin console and a static public site\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.12\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"fastapi>=0.115\",\n    \"uvicorn[standard]>=0.32\",\n    \"jinja2>=3.1\",\n    \"python-multipart>=0.0.12\",   # form posts\n    \"itsdangerous>=2.2\",          # signed session cookies\n    \"argon2-cffi>=23.1\",          # password hashing (never store plain passwords)\n    \"markdown-it-py>=3.0\",        # Markdown -> HTML\n    \"nh3>=0.2\",                   # HTML sanitizer (strips <script> etc.)\n    \"ghp-import>=2.1\",            # push site/ to the gh-pages branch\n]\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [\"pytest>=8.3\", \"httpx>=0.28\"]\n\n[project.scripts]\ncms = \"app.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\n\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"hatchling\"]\nbuild-backend = \"hatchling.build\"\n\n[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]\npackages = [\"app\"]\n# ADR-002: FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite, with no frontend framework\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nThe project has to be readable by someone who has never built a web application,\ngradable from a clean clone, and finishable in two weeks on a $20 plan. Options\nconsidered: Django (batteries included, but a large amount of generated code to\nread), Flask (similar to FastAPI here), a JavaScript stack (a second language and\na build step), and FastAPI with server-rendered templates.\n\n## Decision\n\nFastAPI with Jinja2 templates, SQLite for storage, argon2 for password hashing,\nand an HTML sanitizer for user Markdown. No frontend framework, no build step.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Every page is server-rendered, so \"view source\" shows what the code produced,\n  which makes debugging visible.\n- SQLite is a single file, so a grader can seed and inspect it easily. It is\n  gitignored; `scripts/seed_demo.py` rebuilds it.\n- No build step means the file you preview is the file that ships.\n- Interactive flourishes (live preview without reload, drag-and-drop) cost more\n  here than in a JavaScript stack. That is an accepted trade.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"796d0bfe-f9e6-4d3e-9683-c079ff6c4a2e","session_id":"35af8ee5-7e62-41b5-a09d-9972647e2fab","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/yawoppong-krampah/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"ed9c218c-b06e-43fd-889e-ee2e10651622","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
